Your Message

(This same message is properly formatted for visual ease via the attached PDF "1 CCE Navy Message.pdf") Hello, I am demanding immediate escalation and manual reopening of my chargeback for the April 25, 2025 transaction to Credit Counsel Elite for $20,600. Although the system is currently blocking dispute resubmission, this case qualifies for mandatory exception processing under federal law, card network rules, and Navy Federal’s legal obligations under the Fair Credit Billing Act (FCBA). Key Facts: I previously filed this dispute on May 21, 2025 - well within all dispute timeframes. The merchant then coerced me into canceling my dispute through unlawful tactics, including: Threats of legal action. False claims that I had no refund rights. Threats to blacklist me from banks and lenders (which is illegal under FCRA and ECOA). Additionally, the merchant’s conduct constitutes illegal retaliation. They explicitly threatened to blacklist me from financial institutions for exercising my right to a refund and chargeback. This violates: Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA) - Prohibits interference with credit rights. Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA) - Prohibits credit-related retaliation. FTC Act §5 - Prohibits unfair and retaliatory business conduct. Visa Core Rule 11.3.4 - Prohibits merchant retaliation against customers exercising chargeback rights. These unlawful threats were designed to suppress my initial dispute, constituting illegal retaliation and coercion under federal law. The merchant presented their contract only after I had paid, rendering it unenforceable under: Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3) Visa Core Rule 11.3.4 (no merchant can waive chargeback rights). UCC §2-302 (prohibits unconscionable contracts). The merchant failed to deliver the promised services—this qualifies as services not rendered, a protected and enforceable chargeback reason under FCBA, TILA, Visa/Mastercard Rules, and CFPB regulations. My dispute rights are fully preserved under: FCBA (15 U.S.C. §1666) - Billing errors and merchant deception. TILA (15 U.S.C. §1601 et seq.) - Misrepresentation of services. FTC Act §5 (15 U.S.C. §45) - Prohibits deceptive business practices. Visa/Mastercard Network Rules - Forbid denial of chargeback rights. FCRA & ECOA - Forbid threats and credit interference. Common Law Protections Against Fraud & Coercion - The merchant’s contract is voidable and unenforceable. Evidence Provided: Proof I paid before seeing any agreement. Detailed records of merchant threats, misrepresentations, and retaliation. Updated PDF dispute letter and prior evidence packets (submitted in my May dispute). Key Legal Note: My rights to dispute and recover funds remain intact under federal law regardless of system limitations or deadlines because the delay resulted solely from merchant misconduct, coercion, and retaliation. Under the FCBA, Navy Federal is required to apply flexibility in such cases. I am formally demanding this case be immediately escalated to your legal or senior disputes team for manual review and chargeback processing. The facts, legal protections, and evidence here leave no lawful grounds for denial. Please confirm receipt and advise on next steps. Thank you, Alan Nguyen

This action further confirms the lack of any actual deliverables and underscores their pattern of coercive and deceptive behavior. This is a clear violation of the Consumer Review Fairness Act (CRFA), which protects consumers from being penalized or retaliated against for leaving truthful reviews. It also further supports my dispute under the Fair Credit Billing Act (FCBA) and FTC Act §5, as it demonstrates continued bad faith, non-performance, and retaliation against a paying customer. This retaliation has deepened the financial harm and eliminated even the limited access I had, making it even more urgent that this dispute be resolved in my favor. > 1) SUMMARY 
> 
> I paid $20,600 to enroll in a high-ticket financial program called Credit Counsel 
> Elite (CCE). The service was represented in multiple sales calls as a high-touch, 
> done-for-you (DFY) mentorship experience, where I would receive personalized 
> help to secure funding, restructure existing debt, and access urgent 1-on-1 
> support with bank relationships and approvals. 
> 
> However, these core services were never delivered. Support was delayed, 
> inconsistent, and failed to meet the agreed-upon scope. I received no viable 
> funding solutions, and was left to self-direct the entire process. The only 1-on-1 
> call came 12–14 days before a known funding deadline and offered no actionable 
> resolution. No strategic execution or done-for-you deliverables were provided. 
> 
> Additionally, I was not presented with any contract, refund policy, or written 
> terms at the time of payment. These were delivered after funds were collected, 
> in violation of Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3). This means 
> no enforceable agreement was in place at the time of transaction— voiding 
> any post-payment refund restrictions. 
> 
> Furthermore, CCE’s response to my refund request included threats of 
> blacklisting me from financial institutions, and defamatory language implying 
> criminal behavior — a potential violation of the Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA), 
> the Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA), and Federal Trade Commission 
> (FTC) protections against deceptive practices. 
> 
> Per Visa Core Rules §11.3.4, no merchant may enforce a waiver of chargeback 
> rights, and any such clause is void. I am exercising my right under this provision. 
> 
> I am filing this dispute based on: 
> 
> ● Material misrepresentation and omission during the sales process 
> (Contract §164) 
> 
> ● Non-delivery of essential services 
> ● Lack of mutual assent at time of transaction 
> ● Unconscionable and one-sided contract terms (UCC §2-302) 
> ● Post-payment refund terms, unenforceable under card network rules 
> 
> I request a full reversal of the $20,600 charge on the basis of non-receipt of 
> services and post-payment misrepresentation. 
> 
>  
>  
>  
>  
> 
> 
> 
> 2) PROOF OF PAYMENT TIME - FRIDAY APR 25, @**4:42PM** 
> (BEFORE ANY CONTRACTS OR TERMS PROVIDED) 
> 
>  
> 
>  
> 3) PDF CONTRACT PRESENTED **AFTER** PAYMENT - @**4:46PM** 
> 
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
> a. Lack of Mutual Assent at Time of Payment 
> Protection: Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3) 
> 
> A contract requires mutual consent at the time of the transaction. If you pay before seeing or 
> agreeing to key terms (like refund policy or waivers), those terms are unenforceable. 
> 
> 
> 
> Paid before receiving the contract → no valid agreement. 
>  
> 
> b. Voidability Due to Material Misrepresentation or Omission 
> Protection: Restatement (Second) §164 
> 
> Induced by false promises, omissions, and/or misleading framing, rendering the contract voidable, 
> even if signed. 
> 
> They sold the program as “done-for-you” and high-touch — then delivered delayed, hands-off support. That’s 
> misrepresentation. 
> 
>  
> c. Unconscionable Contract Terms 
> Protection: UCC §2-302 
> 
> A contract is unenforceable if it’s “overly harsh, one-sided, or imposed unfairly.” 
> A $30K “no refund” clause introduced after payment, paired with non-performance, is unconscionable. 
> 
>  
> d. Visa and Mastercard Chargeback Rights 
> Protection: Visa Core Rule §11.3.4 / Mastercard Chargeback Guidelines 
> 
> A merchant cannot require or enforce a waiver of chargeback rights. Any such clause is void and 
> unenforceable by both law and card policy. 
> 
> They included language restricting refunds and chargebacks after one paid — it automatically voids that clause. 
>  
> 
> e. FCRA & ECOA Protections (Against Retaliation) 
> Protection: Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA) & Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA) 
> If they: 
> 
> ● Attempt to “blacklist” you from banks 
> ● Threaten to interfere with your ability to get credit 
> 
> Constitutes: 
> - Unlawful interference 
> - Defamation 
> - Violation of federal rights to fair access to credit 
> 
>  
> f. Consumer Fraud and Deceptive Practices Protections 
> Protection: Federal Trade Commission Act (FTC Act §5) 
> 
> It’s illegal to engage in unfair or deceptive acts in commerce — including misleading sales practices 
> and refusal to refund in bad faith. 
> 
> Their misrepresentation and post-payment enforcement of refund denial violates this standard. 
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
> 
>  
> 
> 
> 
>  
>

**CFPB Complaint Draft — Credit Counsel Elite / Navy Federal**

**Company complained about:**  
- *Merchant:* Credit Counsel Elite (Brandon Elliot)  
- *Financial Institution:* Navy Federal Credit Union  

**Product:** Credit Card  
**Issue:** Disputed charge not resolved properly  
**Date of transaction:** April 25, 2025  
**Disputed amount:** $20,600  

---

### **Summary of Complaint:**
I am filing this complaint to report serious violations of federal consumer protection laws by both (1) the merchant Credit Counsel Elite (CCE) and (2) Navy Federal Credit Union (issuer of my credit card).

---

### **Merchant Misconduct Summary:**
I paid $20,600 to CCE based on their explicit promises—verbally and in writing—that I would receive $300,000 in 0% business credit within 30–90 days. I disclosed my financial situation upfront, and they confirmed I was a qualified fit. 

After payment:
- **No real services were delivered.**
- I was forced to self-direct everything with no viable support, no tangible results, and only one unhelpful call right before a funding deadline.
- No contract or refund policy was presented until *after* I paid—rendering their “terms” legally void under Restatement (Second) of Contracts and UCC §2-302.
- They used **coercion** and **threats** to force me into canceling my original dispute: including threats of lawsuits, denial of refunds, and blacklisting from lenders—violating the FCRA, ECOA, and FTC Act §5.
- When I later posted a truthful public review, they **retaliated** by revoking all program access, a clear violation of the **Consumer Review Fairness Act**.

---

### **Credit Card Dispute Mishandling:**
I submitted my original dispute to Navy Federal on May 21, 2025—within all legal timeframes. After CCE’s threats, I canceled the dispute under duress. I later attempted to reopen it based on:
- Clear **fraud**, **non-delivery**, and **illegal coercion**.
- Federal law requiring flexibility when merchant misconduct causes delay (see: FCBA, TILA, FTC Act §5, CFPB Guidance).
- Visa Core Rules §11.3.4 prohibits merchants from retaliating or forcing waiver of chargeback rights.

Despite overwhelming evidence, Navy Federal has refused to manually escalate or process my dispute—citing arbitrary “system limitations.” They are now unlawfully blocking my chargeback rights in direct violation of the **Fair Credit Billing Act (15 U.S.C. §1666)** and **CFPB rules (12 U.S.C. §5531)**.

---

### **Applicable Violations:**

**By Credit Counsel Elite:**
- **FTC Act §5:** Deceptive and unfair practices (bait-and-switch, false results, retaliatory access denial)
- **FCBA / TILA (15 U.S.C. §§1666, 1601):** Misrepresentation, services not rendered, refund refusal
- **Consumer Review Fairness Act:** Revoking access in retaliation for a truthful review
- **FCRA / ECOA:** Threats to blacklist from lenders, illegal retaliation
- **Restatement of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3); UCC §2-302:** Post-payment terms, unconscionable contracts
- **Visa Core Rule 11.3.4:** Waiver of chargeback rights is void

**By Navy Federal Credit Union:**
- **FCBA (15 U.S.C. §1666):** Failure to uphold chargeback rights due to merchant deception
- **CFPB Rules (12 U.S.C. §5531):** Failure to investigate, obstructing valid consumer dispute
- **TILA / FTC Act §5:** Failing to enforce protections for fraud and misrepresentation

---

### **Requested Resolution:**
- Immediate **chargeback and full refund of $20,600** via Navy Federal, due to:
  - Services not rendered
  - Coercion, misrepresentation, and retaliation
- Enforcement actions against CCE for multiple federal violations
- Investigation into Navy Federal’s systemic denial of federally protected chargeback rights

---

### **Supporting Evidence Available Upon Request:**
- Payment timestamps vs. contract delivery
- Recordings and emails showing misrepresentation and coercion
- Retaliation screenshots from CCE
- FTC complaint confirmation (#190055885)
- PDF dispute letters and evidence packets

I paid $20,600 upfront based on the merchant’s clear verbal and written promises that I would receive $300,000+ in 0% business credit within 30–90 days. - Threatening legal action
- Illegally threatening to “blacklist” me from banks and lenders (a violation of the FCRA and ECOA)

This created undue fear and caused me to withdraw the dispute under duress. I later learned these were unlawful threats and misrepresentations. When I posted a truthful review about my experience, they retaliated by revoking all access to the program despite full payment—violating the **Consumer Review Fairness Act.**

**I am now requesting Navy Federal reprocess my dispute under the following federal protections:**

- **Fair Credit Billing Act (15 U.S.C. §1666):** Allows disputes for services not rendered and requires flexibility when merchant misconduct delays action.
- **Truth in Lending Act (15 U.S.C. §1601 et seq.):** Protects against misrepresentation and failed delivery.
- **CFPB Regulation (12 U.S.C. §5531):** Prohibits the suppression of valid disputes caused by deceptive or abusive merchant behavior.
- **Visa Core Rule 11.3.4:** Forbids merchants from retaliating or waiving customer chargeback rights.

My goal is to obtain cooperation from Navy Federal to escalate this dispute manually and reverse the transaction based on overwhelming evidence of fraud, misrepresentation, and retaliation by the merchant. I have already provided detailed proof, including:

  - Threatened legal action
  - Claimed no refunds were allowed

- Out of fear, I withdrew the dispute. I now understand this was **unlawful coercion and retaliation**.

---

- **Consumer Review Fairness Act:** Retaliation for honest review  
- **FCRA & ECOA:** Threatening blacklisting from credit access

> 
> Background & Timeline: 
> 
> 1. I paid this merchant before any agreements or contracts were sent or signed - a 
> fundamental violation of fair business practices. 
>  
> 
> 2. Documentation - including payment timestamps and communications - was already 
> provided in my previous dispute with you, proving this fact. 
>  
> 
> 3. I clearly communicated to the merchant before purchasing that I was only willing to join 
> if they could confidently help my specific situation. They assured me, with certainty 
> and confidence, that I would receive $300,000+ in 0% business credit within 30 to 90 
> days, consistent with their advertising on their website and marketing materials. 
>  
> 
> 4. After paying, it became clear they were unable or unwilling to fulfill these promises. No 
> meaningful services were provided to help me obtain this funding, despite my active 
> participation and cooperation. 
>  
> 
> 5. I submitted a refund request directly to the merchant, followed by a formal dispute with 
> Navy Federal around May 21, 2025 - approximately 30 days after the purchase. 
>  
> 
> 6. Immediately after filing the dispute, the merchant coerced me into canceling it by: 
>  
> 
> ○ Telling me I wasn’t eligible for a refund (which was false). 
>  
> 
> ○ Threatening me with legal action, claiming they had lawyers ready to pursue me. 
>  
> 
> 
> 
> ○ Threatening to call banks and lenders to have me “blacklisted” from future credit 
> opportunities. 
>  
> 
> 7. Out of fear of legal and financial retaliation, I reluctantly canceled my dispute. 
>  
> 
> 8. I have since learned these actions were misleading, deceptive, and coercive. The 
> merchant intentionally blocked my refund and dispute rights through intimidation and 
> misrepresentation. 
>  
> 
> 9. Despite repeated requests and time given, they still failed to deliver the services I paid 
> for. 
>  
> 
>  
> 
> Basis for This Dispute: 
> 
> ● Services not rendered / Misrepresentation of results 
>  
> 
> ● Merchant coercion and threats 
>  
> 
> ● Improper payment collection before agreement / deceptive sales tactics 
>  
> 
> ● Previous dispute filed promptly with supporting documentation 
>  
> 
> ● Delay in this chargeback caused solely by merchant misconduct, not by inaction 
> on my part 
> 
> Legal Basis for Dispute & Rights Preservation: 
> 
> This dispute is fully protected under federal law, regardless of timeline, due to the nature 
> of the merchant’s actions. I am asserting my rights under the following statutes and 
> protections: 
> 
> 1. Fair Credit Billing Act (FCBA, 15 U.S.C. §1666): 
>  This law protects cardholders from billing errors and services not rendered. It 
> allows disputes for services that were not delivered as promised or were 
> misrepresented. Though it generally provides a 60-day dispute window, it 
> explicitly allows flexibility when merchant deception or coercion delayed filing. My 
> original dispute was filed promptly, and merchant coercion caused the delay in 
> final resolution. 
>  
> 
> 2. Truth in Lending Act (TILA, 15 U.S.C. §1601 et seq.): 
>  This act enforces my right to dispute any charge involving misrepresentation or 
> 
> 
> 
> non-delivery of services. 
>  
> 
> 3. FTC Act – Section 5 (15 U.S.C. §45): 
>  Prohibits unfair or deceptive business practices. The merchant engaged in 
> deceptive practices by falsely promising guaranteed results and coercing me to 
> cancel my dispute under legal threats and false claims of non-refundable status. 
>  
> 
> 4. Consumer Financial Protection Bureau (CFPB) Regulations (12 U.S.C. §5531): 
>  Require banks to investigate disputes fairly and thoroughly, and prohibit 
> suppression of valid consumer disputes resulting from deceptive merchant 
> conduct. 
>  
> 
> 5. Common Law Protections Against Fraudulent Inducement & Coercion: 
>  Any agreement formed under false promises or coercive tactics is legally 
> voidable. The merchant’s conduct nullifies any “agreements” formed after 
> payment. 
>  
> 
> Due to these protections, I retain the full right to this chargeback and refund regardless 
> of any timelines the merchant or others may claim. 
> 
>  
> 
>  
>

> 
> ● Refund request communications 
>  
> 
> ● Documentation showing coercion and threats 
>  
>

>

> my consumer rights.

> Alan Nguyen

> [!abstract]- Web browsing
> CCE_Refund_Dispute_Evidence_AlanNguyen
> 
> 
> 1) SUMMARY 
> 
> I paid $20,600 to enroll in a high-ticket financial program called Credit Counsel 
> Elite (CCE). The service was represented in multiple sales calls as a high-touch, 
> done-for-you (DFY) mentorship experience, where I would receive personalized 
> help to secure funding, restructure existing debt, and access urgent 1-on-1 
> support with bank relationships and approvals. 
> 
> However, these core services were never delivered. Support was delayed, 
> inconsistent, and failed to meet the agreed-upon scope. I received no viable 
> funding solutions, and was left to self-direct the entire process. The only 1-on-1 
> call came 12–14 days before a known funding deadline and offered no actionable 
> resolution. No strategic execution or done-for-you deliverables were provided. 
> 
> Additionally, I was not presented with any contract, refund policy, or written 
> terms at the time of payment. These were delivered after funds were collected, 
> in violation of Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3). This means 
> no enforceable agreement was in place at the time of transaction— voiding 
> any post-payment refund restrictions. 
> 
> Furthermore, CCE’s response to my refund request included threats of 
> blacklisting me from financial institutions, and defamatory language implying 
> criminal behavior — a potential violation of the Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA), 
> the Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA), and Federal Trade Commission 
> (FTC) protections against deceptive practices. 
> 
> Per Visa Core Rules §11.3.4, no merchant may enforce a waiver of chargeback 
> rights, and any such clause is void. I am exercising my right under this provision. 
> 
> I am filing this dispute based on: 
> 
> ● Material misrepresentation and omission during the sales process 
> (Contract §164) 
> 
> ● Non-delivery of essential services 
> ● Lack of mutual assent at time of transaction 
> ● Unconscionable and one-sided contract terms (UCC §2-302) 
> ● Post-payment refund terms, unenforceable under card network rules 
> 
> I request a full reversal of the $20,600 charge on the basis of non-receipt of 
> services and post-payment misrepresentation. 
> 
>  
>  
>  
>  
> 
> 
> 
> 2) PROOF OF PAYMENT TIME - FRIDAY APR 25, @**4:42PM** 
> (BEFORE ANY CONTRACTS OR TERMS PROVIDED) 
> 
>  
> 
>  
> 3) PDF CONTRACT PRESENTED **AFTER** PAYMENT - @**4:46PM** 
> 
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
> a. Lack of Mutual Assent at Time of Payment 
> Protection: Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3) 
> 
> A contract requires mutual consent at the time of the transaction. If you pay before seeing or 
> agreeing to key terms (like refund policy or waivers), those terms are unenforceable. 
> 
> 
> 
> Paid before receiving the contract → no valid agreement. 
>  
> 
> b. Voidability Due to Material Misrepresentation or Omission 
> Protection: Restatement (Second) §164 
> 
> Induced by false promises, omissions, and/or misleading framing, rendering the contract voidable, 
> even if signed. 
> 
> They sold the program as “done-for-you” and high-touch — then delivered delayed, hands-off support. That’s 
> misrepresentation. 
> 
>  
> c. Unconscionable Contract Terms 
> Protection: UCC §2-302 
> 
> A contract is unenforceable if it’s “overly harsh, one-sided, or imposed unfairly.” 
> A $30K “no refund” clause introduced after payment, paired with non-performance, is unconscionable. 
> 
>  
> d. Visa and Mastercard Chargeback Rights 
> Protection: Visa Core Rule §11.3.4 / Mastercard Chargeback Guidelines 
> 
> A merchant cannot require or enforce a waiver of chargeback rights. Any such clause is void and 
> unenforceable by both law and card policy. 
> 
> They included language restricting refunds and chargebacks after one paid — it automatically voids that clause. 
>  
> 
> e. FCRA & ECOA Protections (Against Retaliation) 
> Protection: Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA) & Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA) 
> If they: 
> 
> ● Attempt to “blacklist” you from banks 
> ● Threaten to interfere with your ability to get credit 
> 
> Constitutes: 
> - Unlawful interference 
> - Defamation 
> - Violation of federal rights to fair access to credit 
> 
>  
> f. Consumer Fraud and Deceptive Practices Protections 
> Protection: Federal Trade Commission Act (FTC Act §5) 
> 
> It’s illegal to engage in unfair or deceptive acts in commerce — including misleading sales practices 
> and refusal to refund in bad faith. 
> 
> Their misrepresentation and post-payment enforcement of refund denial violates this standard. 
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
> 
>  
> 
> 
> 
>  
> 
> 3) RETALIATION & ILLEGAL THREATS 
> 
> This message from the merchant shows a clear refusal to issue a refund and includes retaliatory 
> threats, including blacklisting me from banks and equating my refund request with "terrorism." 
> These actions violate consumer protection laws and card network policies, including: 
> 
> ● FCRA & ECOA – Attempting to interfere with access to credit through blacklisting 
> ● FTC Act §5 – Unfair and deceptive business practices 
> ● Visa Core Rule 11.3.4 – Prohibits merchants from restricting chargeback rights 
> 
> This communication demonstrates bad faith, non-delivery of promised services, and legal 
> violations. Submitted as supporting documentation for chargeback review. 
> 
>  
> 
>  
> 
>  
> 
> “I will notify our bank connections that will therefore blacklist you from each of these banks.” 
> 
>  
> 
>  
> 
>  
>  
>  
> 
> “We don’t negotiate with terrorists.” 
> 
>  
>  
>  
> I am exercising my right under consumer and payment protection law in good faith. I 
> respectfully request that this transaction be reversed immediately to prevent further 
> harm and financial exposure.  
> 
> 
> 	a. Lack of Mutual Assent at Time of Payment 
> 	b. Voidability Due to Material Misrepresentation or Omission 
> 	c. Unconscionable Contract Terms 
> 	d. Visa and Mastercard Chargeback Rights

> What happened
> 
> Nature: Misrepresentation, Non-Delivery, Retaliation, Refund Refusal, Unlawful Practices I am filing this complaint to report unlawful
> 
> conduct by Credit Counsel Elite and its principal, Brandon Elliot. I paid $20,600 upfront based on their verbal and written claims that I
> 
> would be obtaining $300,000+ in 0% business funding within 30-90 days. I clearly disclosed my unique financial circumstances in
> 
> advance, and they assured me that I will confidently be able to achieve that goal given the specifics discussed. After payment was
> 
> submitted, before any contract was sent or signed; there was a total failure of promised delivery. Key Facts: I received no actual
> 
> funding, no tailored support, and no meaningful help aligned to my stated constraints. CCE’s “coaching” consisted of generic bank
> 
> lists, outdated data, and DIY tasks I have already obtained online for free. They try to disguise this as "proprietary data" when it is
> 
> offered freely online. Their "deeply discounted" “Authorized User” tradeline marketplace was falsely advertised - priced 2x higher
> 
> than both public and private market rates. Their data caused multiple applications to be flagged, triggering unnecessary income
> 
> verification - the opposite of what they guaranteed. Misleading. They offered no real funding partners, only a handful of alleged
> 
> “RMs” (many unresponsive or ineffective). After asking for a refund, they illegally threatened to call the banks and blacklist me from
> 
> services. When I posted a truthful public review, they illegally retaliated by revoking all access, cutting me off from anything I paid for.
> 
> Documented FTC Violations (All Enforceable): FTC Act §5 (Deceptive Practices): - False and unsubstantiated claims about results -
> 
> Bait-and-switch offers (e.g., deep AU discounts) - Misrepresenting capabilities and success metrics FTC Enforcement of FCBA (15
> 
> U.S.C. §1666): - Illegally refusing refunds for undelivered services - “All sales final” language does not override federal law Negative
> 
> Option Rule / ROSCA Violations: - Automatically enforcing terms without affirmative, informed consent - Collecting payment prior to
> 
> agreement delivery - Retaliation & Unfair Business Practices: - Revoking access after leaving a truthful, verified review - Retaliatory
> 
> blacklisting and verbal discussion of collections and using their legal team Unlawful Contract Practices (Restatement Contracts §§17,
> 
> 22; UCC §2-302): - Payment was made without mutual assent, rendering contract void - No real opportunity to negotiate or decline
> 
> terms post-payment Why Refund Is Required Under FTC Authority: The FTC requires all representations be truthful, substantiated,
> 
> and non-deceptive. Any contract is void if obtained through misrepresentation. “All sales final” is unenforceable when services are
> 
> never rendered or fail to match advertised claims. CCE failed to provide any tangible deliverables matching their promises. I was
> 
> misled, harmed financially, and retaliated against for speaking truthfully - a clear violation of both FTC and Visa/Mastercard policies
> 
> prohibiting retaliation. Requested Remedy: I request a full $20,600 refund due everything above, incuding: Material

**Subject:** Additional Federal Complaint Evidence — Urgent Escalation Request for Immediate Refund (CCE Transaction 04/25/2025 - $20,600)

Dear Navy Federal Disputes Team,

I am submitting this message as a formal escalation and supplemental evidence submission regarding the April 25, 2025, $20,600 transaction to *Credit Counsel Elite* (CCE), for which I previously filed a chargeback.

Attached are:
- My **federal CFPB complaint** submitted against the merchant  
- A supporting evidence PDF containing detailed documentation of **misrepresentation, fraud, coercion, retaliation**, and **service non-performance**

---

### ✅ **Key Compliance Points:**

This case qualifies for **mandatory refund** under multiple federal laws and card network regulations:

---

**1. Fair Credit Billing Act (FCBA) – 15 U.S.C. §1666**  
> Protects against billing errors, services not rendered, and misrepresented charges.  
> Timeline flexibility applies **when merchant deception or coercion delayed the dispute.**  
> My original dispute was filed timely; withdrawal was due to illegal threats, which preserves all rights under this law.

---

**2. Truth in Lending Act (TILA) – 15 U.S.C. §1601 et seq.**  
> Enforces consumer rights when services materially differ from what was advertised or promised.  
> No funding or tangible deliverables were ever provided—this is clear misrepresentation.

---

**3. FTC Act §5 – 15 U.S.C. §45**  
> Prohibits deceptive and unfair business practices.  
> CCE’s behavior (bait-and-switch offers, false guarantees, and retaliation) constitutes multiple violations.

---

**4. Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA) & Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA)**  
> The merchant **threatened to blacklist me with lenders and banks**—a clear violation of federal credit protection laws.  
> This was **documented, deliberate coercion**, and directly interfered with my access to fair credit.

---

**5. Consumer Review Fairness Act**  
> CCE revoked my platform access after I posted a truthful public review—**illegal retaliation** under federal statute.

---

**6. Visa Core Rule 11.3.4**  
> Explicitly prohibits merchants from **waiving or suppressing chargeback rights** or retaliating against cardholders.

---

### 📌 **Attached Documentation Proves:**
- Payment was made *before* any contract or terms were presented  
- Merchant issued **threats of legal action and blacklisting** to force cancellation of my initial dispute  
- **No services were delivered** that matched the promise or scope  
- Merchant **revoked access** after I left a verified public review  
- Ads clearly **misrepresented** the service (proof included)

---

### ⚖️ **Requested Action:**

➡️ **Immediate refund and chargeback reversal of $20,600** for the April 25, 2025 transaction

You now have everything needed to process this claim under **mandatory exception handling** and **senior manual review.**

## 🤖 ChatGPT

### ✅ You *can* post a YouTube video — **if done carefully and truthfully.**

Here’s the legal breakdown:

---

## 🔒 **Your Legal Rights:**

### 1. **Truth = Absolute Defense to Defamation**
- If you **state only verifiable facts**, not exaggerations or lies, **you’re protected**.
- Example: “He promised $300K in funding, I paid $20,600, received nothing, then got kicked out.”
- **Backed by evidence = bulletproof.**

---

### 2. **Consumer Review Fairness Act (CRFA) – 15 U.S.C. §45b**
- **Void any contract clause** that prohibits or penalizes public criticism.
- Even if the contract says “no disparagement,” it **cannot be legally enforced**.

---

### 3. **First Amendment**
- You have the right to speak **your honest experience** publicly.
- This includes **reviews, opinions, and factual timelines.**

---

## ⚠️ Watch Out For:

### ❌ Defamation
- Avoid **false claims, speculation about intent, or attacks on character**.
  - ❌ “He’s a scam artist” → risky  
  - ✅ “He made promises, took my money, and didn’t deliver” → safe  

### ❌ Misuse of Private Information
- Don’t share his **home address**, **personal info**, or **recorded calls** without consent unless in a **one-party consent state**.

### ❌ Breach of Confidentiality
- If the contract includes an NDA or confidentiality clause, get it reviewed.  
  - But again — **CRFA overrides most gag clauses**.

---

## ✅ Safe Script Formula:
1. **State the timeline**
2. **Show proof of payment and promises**
3. **Explain what was promised vs delivered**
4. **Avoid name-calling or intent assumptions**
5. **Invite others to share their stories**
6. **Mention FTC/CFPB/AG complaints filed**

---

### Verdict:
**Yes, you can post.** Just keep it:
- **Fact-based**
- **Documented**
- **Non-emotional**
- **Without private info**

## 🎤 **TRUTHFUL EXPOSURE FRAMEWORK**  
**Purpose:** Protect others. Speak your truth. Stay legally safe.

---

### 1. **HOOK** (Start with Impact — Grab Attention)

- "I paid $20,000 to a program that promised me $300,000 in business funding."
- "What happened next changed how I see mentorship forever."

---

### 2. **EXPECTATION SETUP** (What You Were Told)

- Share the promise:
  - "They promised I’d get over $300K in 0% business credit."
  - "They made it sound guaranteed — 30 to 90 days tops."
- Speak on how it was sold to you (tone, urgency, confidence).

---

### 3. **THE REALITY** (What Actually Happened)

- Be clear:
  - "After I paid, I was told to go into banks and say I made over $1M — even if I didn’t."
  - "They coached us to inflate numbers, say we had funding lined up — all to get access to higher-tier bank managers."
- Use phrases like:
  - “This is what I was personally told…”
  - “This was part of the strategy they gave us…”

---

### 4. **THE RED FLAGS** (Deception / Retaliation)

- "I wasn’t shown a contract until after payment."
- "When I asked for a refund or tried to dispute it, they threatened me."
- "I posted a truthful review — they kicked me out, revoked access, and ghosted me."

---

### 5. **HOW IT FELT** (Emotional Truth)

- Let people in:
  - “I felt trapped. Lied to. And deeply regretful for trusting them.”
  - “I would never treat another human the way I was treated.”

---

### 6. **THE TURNING POINT** (Why You're Speaking Now)

- "I’m not sharing this out of hate. I’m sharing this because I wish someone had told me."
- "I don’t want anyone else to lose $20K the way I did."
- “You deserve to know the truth before investing in something like this.”

---

### 7. **CLOSING MESSAGE** (Call to Awareness or Action)

- "If this saved even one person from getting scammed, it was worth sharing."
- "Do your due diligence. Ask for contracts first. Don’t fall for pressure."
- "Stay smart. Stay sovereign. Speak up if something feels wrong."

---

## ✅ LEGAL PROTECTION TIPS (Built-In):
- Everything you say is **first-hand**.
- You’re exposing unethical behavior, **not making legal accusations**.
- You’re sharing **your experience, not defaming**.

   ✅ This blocks you from getting your money back via contract unless they agree voluntarily.

2. **Chargeback Waiver:**
   > “Member expressly waives any and all rights to initiate a chargeback…”  
   ⚠️ While this clause **is not always enforceable** under card network rules (Visa/Mastercard), it **will** be used against you if you try to sue or dispute.

## 🤖 ChatGPT

If **CCE did deliver some 1-on-1 and group coaching**, that does **not** automatically defeat your refund case — **but it shifts your argument.** Here's the legal framing:

---

### ✅ You Can Still Win If You Prove:

#### 1. **Material Misrepresentation**
Even if parts were delivered, your contract is **voidable** if you were **induced to pay based on false or exaggerated claims**.

**Key:**
> “CCE promised I’d receive $300,000+ in 0% business funding within 30–90 days with high certainty — this was the *primary reason* I paid. They failed to deliver this core outcome, which renders the agreement void due to fraudulent inducement.”

Use:
- FTC Act §5 (Deceptive practices)  
- Restatement (Second) of Contracts §164 (Misrepresentation makes contract voidable)

---

#### 2. **Partial Performance Doesn’t Justify Full Payment**
If what they delivered was **incomplete, poor quality, or not what was promised**, you can argue:

> “CCE delivered generic coaching, not personalized, actionable mentorship or funding as claimed. This constitutes a failure of consideration and unjust enrichment.”

Use:
- UCC §2-615 (Impracticability)
- Restatement of Contracts §240 (Divisible contracts: partial breach ≠ full payment due)

---

#### 3. **Retaliation and Suppression of Rights**
Even if services started:
- Kicking you out after a review = **retaliation**  
- Coercing chargeback withdrawal = **violation of Visa Rule 11.3.4**  
- Enforcing post-payment contract = **void for lack of mutual assent**

These independently support reversal and refund.

---

### 🛡️ Bottom Line:

**Delivery alone is not enough** if:
- What was delivered was materially different than what was promised,
- The terms were induced by fraud,
- Your rights were suppressed.

You still have a powerful case. Just **pivot your argument** from *“no delivery”* to *“fraudulent inducement, coercion, and material failure of core promises.”*

**Under Restatement (2d) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3) and UCC §2-302**, a contract presented only *after* payment — without mutual assent or opportunity to decline — is **unenforceable and unconscionable**. Any attempt to impose “no refund” or “no chargeback” clauses **after** money was taken is legally invalid.

---

- **Fair Credit Billing Act (15 U.S.C. §1666)**

CCE’s actions clearly violated Visa’s own policies:

- I was verbally promised business funding support — not vague coaching access.

> What happened?
> 
> I am submitting this complaint regarding a $20,600 credit card transaction made on April 25,
> 2025, to Credit Counsel Elite (CCE) - a financial mentorship company that engaged in deceptive
> marketing, failure to deliver promised services, unlawful coercion, and retaliation. Background &
> Timeline: On April 25, 2025, I paid $20,600 to Credit Counsel Elite after they verbally assured
> me with confidence and certainty that I would receive $300,000+ in 0% interest business funding
> within 30-90 days. This was the foundation of my enrollment. However: No contract or refund
> policy was presented at the time of payment. It was only sent after funds cleared - rendering the
> agreement unenforceable under contract law and card network rules. After payment, no
> meaningful services or results were delivered. I was left to self-direct the process, and no real
> fulfillment of the promised offer occurred. Many things were misrepresented such as the
> DFY/DIY nature of the funding program, as well as advertised “deeply discounted AU’s” - where
> these Authorized User accounts in reality are priced at 2x the public and private market rate. On
> May 21, 2025, I filed a dispute with my card issuer (Navy Federal) within the FCBA timeline.
> But shortly after, I was coerced into canceling it by the merchant through: Threats of legal action
> False claims that I had no refund rights Illegal threats to blacklist me with banks and lenders -
> violating FCRA and ECOA. (Documentation proof attached) Out of fear, I canceled the dispute
> under duress. I now understand these were unlawful, retaliatory, and deceptive tactics. When I
> later posted a truthful public review on TrustPilot about my experience, CCE revoked my access
> to their platform immediately; despite full payment - violating the Consumer Review Fairness
> Act. (they have a history of falsely flagging reviews under defamation, and forcing removal of
> reviews if people do not verify their identity in 3 days. If they do reveal their identity; the CCE
> retaliates exactly as described in my documented experience) - This manipulation creates a false
> sense of the truth. Further reiterating Brandon’s & CCE’s deceptive nature. Violations by Credit
> Counsel Elite: FTC Act §5: Deceptive, unfair, and retaliatory business conduct Fair Credit Billing
> Act (15 U.S.C. §1666): Services not rendered; suppression of refund rights through coercion
> Truth in Lending Act (15 U.S.C. §1601): Misrepresentation of services and failure to deliver
> Consumer Review Fairness Act: Revoking access after a truthful review = illegal retaliation
> FCRA & ECOA: Threatening to interfere with my access to credit = prohibited retaliation UCC
> §2-302 & Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3): Contract introduced post-payment
> is void and unconscionable Visa Core Rule 11.3.4: Prohibits merchants from waiving or
> retaliating against chargeback rights Context Regarding Card Issuer (For Reference): While the
> original Navy Federal dispute was closed due to fear and coercion; I have since resubmitted it.
> The delay was caused by CCE’s misconduct. I request support in reinforcing my rights under:
>

> 
> 
> I am submitting this complaint regarding a $20,600 credit card transaction made on April 25, 
> 2025, to Credit Counsel Elite (CCE) - a financial mentorship company that engaged in 
> deceptive marketing, failure to deliver promised services, unlawful coercion, and retaliation. 
> 
>  
> 
> Background & Timeline: 
> 
> On April 25, 2025, I paid $20,600 to Credit Counsel Elite after they verbally assured me with 
> confidence and certainty that I would receive $300,000+ in 0% interest business funding within 
> 30-90 days. This was the foundation of my enrollment. 
> 
> However: 
> 
> ● No contract or refund policy was presented at the time of payment. It was only sent 
> after funds cleared - rendering the agreement unenforceable under contract law and 
> card network rules. 
> 
> ● After payment, no meaningful services or results were delivered. I was left to 
> self-direct the process, and no real fulfillment of the promised offer occurred. 
> 
> ● Many things were misrepresented such as the DFY/DIY nature of the funding program, 
> as well as advertised “deeply discounted AU’s” - where these Authorized User accounts 
> in reality are priced at 2x the public and private market rate. 
> 
> ● On May 21, 2025, I filed a dispute with my card issuer (Navy Federal) within the FCBA 
> timeline. But shortly after, I was coerced into canceling it by the merchant through: 
> 
> ○ Threats of legal action 
> ○ False claims that I had no refund rights 
> ○ Illegal threats to blacklist me with banks and lenders - violating FCRA and 
> 
> ECOA. (Documentation proof attached) 
> ● Out of fear, I canceled the dispute under duress. I now understand these were unlawful, 
> 
> retaliatory, and deceptive tactics. 
> ● When I later posted a truthful public review on TrustPilot about my experience, CCE 
> 
> revoked my access to their platform immediately; despite full payment - violating the 
> Consumer Review Fairness Act. (they have a history of falsely flagging reviews under 
> defamation, and forcing removal of reviews if people do not verify their identity in 3 days. 
> If they do reveal their identity; the CCE retaliates exactly as described in my documented 
> experience) - This manipulation creates a false sense of the truth. Further reiterating 
> Brandon’s & CCE’s deceptive nature. 
> 
>  
> 
> Violations by Credit Counsel Elite: 
> 
> ● FTC Act §5: Deceptive, unfair, and retaliatory business conduct 
> 
> 
> 
> ● Fair Credit Billing Act (15 U.S.C. §1666): Services not rendered; suppression of refund 
> rights through coercion 
> 
> ● Truth in Lending Act (15 U.S.C. §1601): Misrepresentation of services and failure to 
> deliver 
> 
> ● Consumer Review Fairness Act: Revoking access after a truthful review = illegal 
> retaliation 
> 
> ● FCRA & ECOA: Threatening to interfere with my access to credit = prohibited retaliation 
> ● UCC §2-302 & Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3): Contract 
> 
> introduced post-payment is void and unconscionable 
> ● Visa Core Rule 11.3.4: Prohibits merchants from waiving or retaliating against 
> 
> chargeback rights 
> 
>  
> 
> Context Regarding Card Issuer (For Reference): 
> 
> While the original Navy Federal dispute was closed due to fear and coercion; I have since 
> resubmitted it. The delay was caused by CCE’s misconduct. I request support in reinforcing my 
> rights under: 
> 
> ● FCBA (15 U.S.C. §1666) - Timeline flexibility when coercion causes delay 
> ● CFPB Regs (12 U.S.C. §5531) - Protects against suppression of valid disputes 
> ● Visa/Mastercard Rules - Preserve dispute rights regardless of merchant intimidation 
> 
>  
> 
> Requested Outcome: 
> 
> ● Full and immediate refund of $20,600 
> ● Accountability for CCE’s deceptive practices, retaliation, and unlawful coercion 
> ● Documentation added to CCE’s record for enforcement and future consumer protection 
> 
>  
> 
> Evidence Provided: 
> 
> ● Payment timestamps showing payment before contract delivery 
> ● Documented merchant threats and coercion 
> ● FTC complaint confirmation 
> ● Screenshots, recordings, emails, and other supporting files 
> 
> (additional documentation available upon request) 
> 
> This complaint is filed in good faith to prevent continued harm and to ensure enforcement of my 
> federally protected rights. 
> 
>

> [!abstract]- Web browsing
> 00 Navy CFPB
> 
> 
> Subject: Additional Federal Complaint Evidence - Urgent Escalation Request for Immediate 
> Refund (CCE Transaction 04/25/2025 - $20,600) 
> 
> Dear Navy Federal Disputes Team, 
> 
> I am submitting this message as a formal escalation and supplemental evidence submission 
> regarding the April 25, 2025, $20,600 transaction to Credit Counsel Elite (CCE), for which I 
> previously filed a chargeback. 
> 
> Attached are: 
> 
> ● My federal CFPB complaint submitted against the merchant 
> ● A supporting evidence PDF containing detailed documentation of misrepresentation, 
> 
> fraud, coercion, retaliation, and service non-performance 
> 
>  
> 
> Key Compliance Points: 
> 
> This case qualifies for mandatory refund under multiple federal laws and card network 
> regulations: 
> 
>  
> 
> 1. Fair Credit Billing Act (FCBA) - 15 U.S.C. §1666 
> 
> Protects against billing errors, services not rendered, and misrepresented charges. 
> Timeline flexibility applies when merchant deception or coercion delayed the 
> dispute. 
> My original dispute was filed timely; withdrawal was due to illegal threats, which 
> preserves all rights under this law. 
> 
>  
> 
> 2. Truth in Lending Act (TILA) - 15 U.S.C. §1601 et seq. 
> 
> Enforces consumer rights when services materially differ from what was advertised 
> or promised. 
> No funding or tangible deliverables / fulfilment / promised results were ever 
> provided - this is clear misrepresentation. 
> 
>  
> 
> 3. FTC Act §5 – 15 U.S.C. §45 
> 
> 
> 
> Prohibits deceptive and unfair business practices. 
> CCE’s behavior (bait-and-switch offers, false guarantees, and retaliation) 
> constitutes multiple violations. 
> 
>  
> 
> 4. Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA) & Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA) 
> 
> The merchant threatened to blacklist me with lenders and banks - a clear 
> violation of federal credit protection laws. 
> This was documented, deliberate coercion, and directly interfered with my 
> access to fair credit. 
> 
>  
> 
> 5. Consumer Review Fairness Act 
> 
> CCE revoked my platform access after I posted a truthful public review on TrustPilot 
> - illegal retaliation under federal statute. 
> 
>  
> 
> 6. Visa Core Rule 11.3.4 
> 
> Explicitly prohibits merchants from waiving or suppressing chargeback rights or 
> retaliating against cardholders. 
> 
>  
> 
> Attached Documentation Proves: 
> 
> ● Payment was made before any contract or terms were presented 
> ● Merchant issued threats of legal action and blacklisting to force cancellation of my 
> 
> initial dispute 
> ● No services were delivered that matched the promise or scope 
> ● Merchant revoked access after I left a verified public review 
> ● Ads clearly misrepresented the service (proof included) 
> 
>  
> 
> Requested Action: 
> 
> Given the overwhelming legal merit, supporting documentation, and confirmed federal 
> violations, I respectfully request: 
> 
> Immediate refund and chargeback reversal of $20,600 for the April 25, 2025 transaction 
> 
> 
> 
>  
> 
> Legal Standing: 
> 
> I have complied with all timelines and conditions. 
> The only reason this refund has not yet been honored is due to merchant coercion, which 
> federal law explicitly protects against. 
> You now have everything needed to process this claim under mandatory exception handling 
> and senior manual review. 
> 
>  
> 
> Thank you for your time and prompt cooperation. 
> Please confirm receipt and update me once the chargeback has been finalized. 
> 
> Respectfully, 
> Alan Nguyen 
> nguyenalan95@gmail.com 
> (702) 267-7169 
> 
>  
> 
>

> 	Attached Documentation Proves: 
> 	Requested Action:

> What happened
> 
> Nature: Misrepresentation, Non-Delivery, Retaliation, Refund Refusal, Unlawful Practices I am filing this complaint to report unlawful
> 
> conduct by Credit Counsel Elite and its principal, Brandon Elliot. I paid $20,600 upfront based on their verbal and written claims that I
> 
> would be obtaining $300,000+ in 0% business funding within 30-90 days. I clearly disclosed my unique financial circumstances in
> 
> advance, and they assured me that I will confidently be able to achieve that goal given the specifics discussed. After payment was
> 
> submitted, before any contract was sent or signed; there was a total failure of promised delivery. Key Facts: I received no actual
> 
> funding, no tailored support, and no meaningful help aligned to my stated constraints. CCE’s “coaching” consisted of generic bank
> 
> lists, outdated data, and DIY tasks I have already obtained online for free. They try to disguise this as "proprietary data" when it is
> 
> offered freely online. Their "deeply discounted" “Authorized User” tradeline marketplace was falsely advertised - priced 2x higher
> 
> than both public and private market rates. Their data caused multiple applications to be flagged, triggering unnecessary income
> 
> verification - the opposite of what they guaranteed. Misleading. They offered no real funding partners, only a handful of alleged
> 
> “RMs” (many unresponsive or ineffective). After asking for a refund, they illegally threatened to call the banks and blacklist me from
> 
> services. When I posted a truthful public review, they illegally retaliated by revoking all access, cutting me off from anything I paid for.
> 
> Documented FTC Violations (All Enforceable): FTC Act §5 (Deceptive Practices): - False and unsubstantiated claims about results -
> 
> Bait-and-switch offers (e.g., deep AU discounts) - Misrepresenting capabilities and success metrics FTC Enforcement of FCBA (15
> 
> U.S.C. §1666): - Illegally refusing refunds for undelivered services - “All sales final” language does not override federal law Negative
> 
> Option Rule / ROSCA Violations: - Automatically enforcing terms without affirmative, informed consent - Collecting payment prior to
> 
> agreement delivery - Retaliation & Unfair Business Practices: - Revoking access after leaving a truthful, verified review - Retaliatory
> 
> blacklisting and verbal discussion of collections and using their legal team Unlawful Contract Practices (Restatement Contracts §§17,
> 
> 22; UCC §2-302): - Payment was made without mutual assent, rendering contract void - No real opportunity to negotiate or decline
> 
> terms post-payment Why Refund Is Required Under FTC Authority: The FTC requires all representations be truthful, substantiated,
> 
> and non-deceptive. Any contract is void if obtained through misrepresentation. “All sales final” is unenforceable when services are
> 
> never rendered or fail to match advertised claims. CCE failed to provide any tangible deliverables matching their promises. I was
> 
> misled, harmed financially, and retaliated against for speaking truthfully - a clear violation of both FTC and Visa/Mastercard policies
> 
> prohibiting retaliation. Requested Remedy: I request a full $20,600 refund due everything above, incuding: Material
>

> resolution. No strategic execution or done-for-you deliverables were provided. 
>

> terms at the time of payment. These were delivered after funds were collected,

> any post-payment refund restrictions. 
> 
> Furthermore, CCE’s response to my refund request included threats of 
> blacklisting me from financial institutions, and defamatory language implying 
> criminal behavior — a potential violation of the Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA), 
> the Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA), and Federal Trade Commission 
> (FTC) protections against deceptive practices. 
> 
> Per Visa Core Rules §11.3.4, no merchant may enforce a waiver of chargeback 
> rights, and any such clause is void. I am exercising my right under this provision. 
> 
> I am filing this dispute based on: 
> 
> ● Material misrepresentation and omission during the sales process 
> (Contract §164) 
> 
> ● Non-delivery of essential services 
> ● Lack of mutual assent at time of transaction 
> ● Unconscionable and one-sided contract terms (UCC §2-302) 
> ● Post-payment refund terms, unenforceable under card network rules 
> 
> I request a full reversal of the $20,600 charge on the basis of non-receipt of 
> services and post-payment misrepresentation.

>  
>  
>  
> a. Lack of Mutual Assent at Time of Payment 
> Protection: Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3) 
> 
> A contract requires mutual consent at the time of the transaction. If you pay before seeing or 
> agreeing to key terms (like refund policy or waivers), those terms are unenforceable. 
> 
> 
> 
> Paid before receiving the contract → no valid agreement. 
>  
> 
> b. Voidability Due to Material Misrepresentation or Omission 
> Protection: Restatement (Second) §164 
> 
> Induced by false promises, omissions, and/or misleading framing, rendering the contract voidable, 
> even if signed. 
> 
> They sold the program as “done-for-you” and high-touch — then delivered delayed, hands-off support. That’s 
> misrepresentation. 
> 
>  
> c. Unconscionable Contract Terms 
> Protection: UCC §2-302 
> 
> A contract is unenforceable if it’s “overly harsh, one-sided, or imposed unfairly.” 
> A $30K “no refund” clause introduced after payment, paired with non-performance, is unconscionable. 
> 
>  
> d. Visa and Mastercard Chargeback Rights 
> Protection: Visa Core Rule §11.3.4 / Mastercard Chargeback Guidelines 
> 
> A merchant cannot require or enforce a waiver of chargeback rights. Any such clause is void and 
> unenforceable by both law and card policy. 
> 
> They included language restricting refunds and chargebacks after one paid — it automatically voids that clause. 
>  
> 
> e. FCRA & ECOA Protections (Against Retaliation) 
> Protection: Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA) & Equal Credit Opportunity Act (ECOA) 
> If they: 
> 
> ● Attempt to “blacklist” you from banks 
> ● Threaten to interfere with your ability to get credit 
> 
> Constitutes: 
> - Unlawful interference 
> - Defamation 
> - Violation of federal rights to fair access to credit 
> 
>  
> f. Consumer Fraud and Deceptive Practices Protections 
> Protection: Federal Trade Commission Act (FTC Act §5) 
> 
> It’s illegal to engage in unfair or deceptive acts in commerce — including misleading sales practices 
> and refusal to refund in bad faith. 
> 
> Their misrepresentation and post-payment enforcement of refund denial violates this standard. 
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
>  
> 
>  
> 
> 
> 
>  
> 
> 3) RETALIATION & ILLEGAL THREATS 
> 
> This message from the merchant shows a clear refusal to issue a refund and includes retaliatory 
> threats, including blacklisting me from banks and equating my refund request with "terrorism." 
> These actions violate consumer protection laws and card network policies, including: 
> 
> ● FCRA & ECOA – Attempting to interfere with access to credit through blacklisting 
> ● FTC Act §5 – Unfair and deceptive business practices 
> ● Visa Core Rule 11.3.4 – Prohibits merchants from restricting chargeback rights

## 👤 You

with prejudice? that sounds bad no?

## 🤖 ChatGPT

Excellent catch — and you're absolutely right to question it.

### ⚠️ “With prejudice” in legal disputes:
- In **court**, “dismissed with prejudice” means the case **can’t be brought again** — final and binding.
- In a **refund or chargeback context**, it can imply **finality** in your favor, **but** it can be misinterpreted.

---

### ✅ Best Wording Instead:
Use this instead for **maximum clarity and favor**:

> “I respectfully request that the transaction be reversed and refunded in full, with the matter considered resolved in accordance with federal consumer protections and Visa’s rules.”

This:
- Confirms finality in **your** favor.
- Keeps tone professional, cooperative, and compliant.
- Avoids confusion around legal jargon like “with prejudice.”

Would you like me to insert this into your final version and recompile everything flawlessly?

Merchant: Credit Counsel Elite (CCE), San Diego, CA
Amount: $20,600
Card Issuer: Navy Federal Credit Union
Dear Navy Federal Disputes Team,
This letter provides the complete and itemized response to your July 21, 2025 request for additional documentation related to the above-referenced dispute. Each item requested is addressed below in exact sequence, with references to the attached evidence and prior submissions.
Per Regulation Z (12 CFR §1026.13), once the cardholder provides supporting documentation for a billing error; particularly involving non-delivery or misrepresentation of services; the bank is required to conduct a good-faith investigation. 
All evidentiary burdens under Regulation Z have been met. This response includes a complete timeline, itemized violations, and substantiated proof of non-delivery, misrepresentation, and coercion - warranting immediate reversal under federal and Visa rules.

A. Correspondence with the Merchant Regarding Cancellation
Evidence:
1 CCE Navy Message.pdf
2 CCE Dispute 2.pdf
Screenshot and message documentation showing CCE (Brandon) threatening legal action and blacklisting if I did not cancel the dispute.
Summary:
Merchant coerced me into canceling the chargeback under threats and intimidation. This includes written threats of lawsuits, misstatements that chargebacks are illegal, and blacklisting threats - all violations of Visa Rule 11.3.4 and FCRA/ECOA.
This violates Visa Core Rules 11.3.4.1 (Merchant Threats or Coercion), and 11.3.1 (Misrepresentation or Non-Receipt of Services), both grounds for immediate chargeback support.

B. Terms and Conditions Surrounding the Purchase
Evidence:
Attached 3 04.25.2025 CCE_Refund_Dispute_Evidence_AlanNguyen.pdf
Summary:
The service agreement was only sent after the payment was processed.
Payment timestamp: April 25, 2025, 4:42 PM
Contract sent: April 25, 2025, 4:46 PM
This violates Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§17, 22, 211(3) and UCC §2-302.
No agreement was reviewed, signed, or agreed to before the $20,600 was paid.
Because the contract was sent only after payment, no mutual assent was formed at the time of sale. Moreover, CCE’s failure to deliver the core result constitutes a fundamental breach, voiding any subsequent attempt to enforce the agreement or disclaim liability.
Attempting to impose waiver of rights after purchase constitutes deceptive conduct under FTC UDAP standards. The agreement’s attempt to retroactively bind or disclaim rights post-transaction is legally unenforceable and unconscionable under UCC §2-302.
A contract presented after payment is not enforceable as mutual assent was never established at the time of purchase (Restatement (Second) of Contracts §§ 17, 22; UCC § 2-302).
CCE weaponized a post-payment contract to strip away rights, a tactic considered “unconscionable” under both UCC and federal consumer protection.

C. Correspondence Regarding Attempts to Resolve
Evidence:
1 CCE Navy Message.pdf
2 CCE Dispute 2.pdf
CCE FTC ReportFraud.ftc.gov - Confirmation.pdf
00 CCE CFPB.pdf and CCE CFPB Complaint Detail.pdf
Summary:
I made multiple attempts to resolve directly and professionally.
The merchant denied refunds; due to bait n switch manipulation and deceptive tactics.
The merchant ignored good-faith outreach and instead retaliated with threats and access revocation.
I also filed formal complaints with the CFPB, FTC, and CA Attorney General due to nonresponse and misconduct.

D. Detailed Description of What Was Purchased vs. What Was Received
Purchased:
Explicit promise of $300,000+ in 0% business credit within 30–90 days, with mentorship and 1 on 1 dedicated support.
DFY (Done For You) Funding, double confirmed across multiple reps.
Timely responses
Expedited assistance given precommunicated timeline constraints, of the first 2 weeks, and the first month. (Assurance given that goals would be achieved, and that I would be supported.
Deeply discounted AU’s (Authorized User Tradlines)
9100+ list of connections
Received:
No DFY funding. Bait n Switch to DIY (Do it yourself)  funding; which defeats the purpose of paying premium prices, because DIY models are everywhere for free on the internet, and most certainly not proprietary exclusive to CCE.
AU’s were actually over 2x market and private rates.
No business credit results.
No fulfillment of the core offer.
Connections were less than 10 total, and obsolete.
Very slow responses spanning 2-3+ days with very low effort, often lacking true attention to respond to the core issues discussed at hand. Just seemingly rushed over and not properly addressed.
A DIY program that, in some segments, encourages questionable tactics and potential misrepresentations to institutions - tactics I found ethically unacceptable and legally risky.
Slow responses, with no regard or care to pre communicated timelines that were clearly communicated as something that needed to be addressed in order to sign up. 
Summary:
Deliverables were promised with confidence guarantees but never fulfilled.
Even the offered “coaching” was abruptly revoked after I posted a review.

E. Detailed Description of How the Service Was Not As Described
Evidence:
2 CCE Dispute 2.pdf (timeline and breakdown)
CFPB complaint, FTC confirmation
Summary:
CCE falsely marketed a guaranteed funding system and access, then failed to deliver any measurable funding progress.
The service’s core result was misrepresented and never delivered.
CCE’s conduct meets the definition of merchant fraud under Visa Core Rule 11.3.1 - covering misrepresentation and non-performance of promised deliverables. The dispute therefore meets Visa’s requirements for immediate chargeback.

F. Detailed Timeline of Events
April 25, 2025 @ 4:42 PM - $20,600 paid via Visa (NFCU)
April 25, 2025 @ 4:46 PM - Service agreement sent (post-payment)
April 26–May 18, 2025 - Attempted to follow up; no funding success provided
May 21, 2025 - Original chargeback filed due to non-delivery
Late May 2025 - Merchant threatened me, coerced chargeback cancellation, revoked platform access. Regranted access upon confirmation of closing charge back.
June 2025 - Attempted resolution as merchant is not delivering the $300K of funding in 30-90 days as originally promised and agreed upon.
July 2025 - Posted an honest and factual public review on TrustPilot in good faith, and merchant retaliated by revoking all access and communication. Refiled dispute after all internal resolution efforts failed
Now - Merchant remains unresponsive and continues to withhold all promised deliverables & access; as it has become clear that merchant never was going to deliver in the first place given their deceptive nature.
These events reflect a pattern of calculated deception, not service failure. The merchant’s conduct shows a premeditated intent to mislead, intimidate, and obstruct any accountability.
Pattern of misconduct shows premeditated bait-and-switch, coordinated denial of refund, and intentional revocation of services to suppress complaints. Retaliation for review demonstrates bad faith.

G. Services Received vs. Services Not Provided / Misrepresented
Received:
group coaching sessions, 1-on-1s, no business credit fulfillment
These are minimal and not the core offer sold
Not Received:
Business funding, credit repair, vendor/trade lines at 0%, 
The advertised core result of "$300K+ at 0% within 30-90 days" was never delivered
Deeply discounted AU’s did not exist
Timely assistance relative to predisclosed timelines not addressed (no 1 on 1 until 1 day before deadline, numbing 2 weeks of lack of assistance as promised)
Summary:
CCE failed to provide the agreed-upon value or outcome, voiding the basis of the transaction.
Any claim of partial service is immaterial. Under both Visa rules and contract law, partial or token delivery does not excuse fraudulent inducement or failure to fulfill the core purpose of the transaction. This was not a subscription or exploratory offer - it was a result-based contract.

Additional Reinforcements
CFPB Case #240426-14545424 and filing documents included
FTC Fraud Report Confirmation included
Visa Rule 11.3.4 violations documented clearly in both the letters and the CFPB complaint

Legal Violations Summary

The following legal violations demonstrate irrefutable cause for chargeback and regulatory enforcement:
Visa Rule 11.3.4.1 - Threats and coercion to revoke or block chargebacks.
FTC Act §5 - Misrepresentation of services (bait-and-switch and deceptive marketing).
UCC §2-302 - Unconscionable contract post-payment.
Restatement §211(3) - Hidden terms sprung after payment, not mutually agreed.
FCRA/ECOA - Retaliation and coercion, undermining good-faith financial decisions.
Contract Law - No meeting of the minds, no offer and acceptance pre-payment.

Visa Rule 11.3.4.2 - Chargeback remains valid even if merchant references a signed contract when results are misrepresented or undelivered.

Conclusion and Request
This dispute meets all criteria for a full chargeback due to:
Material misrepresentation
Non-delivery of promised core services
Post-payment coercion and contract invalidation
Clear violations of Visa and federal consumer protections
This transaction is not a mere customer dissatisfaction - it is a textbook example of deceptive business practices designed to circumvent consumer protection laws, and I trust Navy Federal will uphold those laws.

I respectfully request that a chargeback be initiated and a full credit of $20,600 be issued immediately, in accordance with Visa Core Rules and federal consumer protection laws.

Please confirm receipt and let me know if anything further is required.

All supporting evidence has been provided. No further documentation is reasonably available. I respectfully request that this dispute be escalated for final resolution without further delay.

Sincerely,
Alan Nguyen
Phone: 702 267 7169
Email: nguyenalan95@gmail.com

Dear Alan,
We’ve dedicated significant time to thoughtfully review your concerns to ensure we provide a thorough and accurate response. We also want to apologize if this reply was delayed, your message was initially filtered into our spam folder, and we only recently became aware of it. Our goal is to bring clarity and alignment by the end of this email so that everyone is on the same page moving forward.
You joined our program on April 25, 2025, with great enthusiasm to be part of this community. According to your profile, you have completed nearly all of the proprietary course content and materials provided. Additionally, we offered a payment plan to accommodate your needs, though this is not a standard option. Your signed agreement was for a total investment of $30,000, with an initial deposit of $20,000 and two subsequent payments of $5,000 each due on May 25, 2025 and June 25, 2025.
We recognize the emotion in your message, and we don’t take your situation lightly. That’s why we want to respond with transparency, care, and respect.
When you enrolled, you signed the Exclusive Inner Circle Agreement, which clearly explains that due to the nature of the program, including instant access to proprietary methods, tools, coaching, and community that all sales are final and strictly non-refundable. You signed and initialed this on page 3 of the agreement. It also states that any attempt to file a chargeback would be a breach of that agreement.
On May 22, 2025, just days before your next scheduled payment, you met on a zoom call with Brandon to talk about previous warnings. During that meeting, you acknowledged the no-refund policy again, apologized for earlier actions, and promised to show up with your best behavior moving forward. You also stated you understood that results come from your own action, not from guarantees and that it can take up to 6 months for the results you were desiring. 
It’s also worth noting that after this call, you had scheduled a Zoom call with Brandon and did not show up. A second Zoom was scheduled where you were late, but thanks to our team’s follow-up and your eventual attendance, that conversation was able to happen. You also missed multiple expert coaching calls that were available to support your progress. Despite these missed opportunities, the fact remains that after joining our program, you successfully obtained $80,000 in funding, a result many members would be proud of. While individual outcomes vary, this demonstrates what’s possible when the strategies are followed and action is taken.
Out of trust, respect, and good faith, we gave you another chance to continue in the program based on your word after you apologized and promised to re-engage respectfully. Unfortunately, those commitments were not upheld.
We want to be clear that you made an empowered decision to join the Inner Circle program, knowing it was a premium option designed to provide you with powerful tools, direct coaching, and a high-level peer community. No one forced you to join. We didn’t pressure or mislead you as your enrollment was entirely your decision, and we welcomed you with full access and support from day one. While we provide our best tools, guidance, and support, just like with any online educational program or anything in life, results are never guaranteed. It’s always up to each member to take full ownership, follow the process, put in consistent effort and stay positively engaged to achieve their desired outcome.
Our leadership and fulfillment team reviewed everything carefully. Based on your signed agreement, your activity, and the internal resources dedicated to your membership, we are standing by the terms you agreed to. Issuing a refund at this stage would go against our policies and would be unfair to every member who followed the process and upheld their commitment.
For your reference, here is our Refund Policy, which has been fully transparent from the start and was clearly outlined in the agreement you reviewed, acknowledged, signed, and initialed, as follows:
￼
In addition, the agreement gives us the right to end a membership if someone becomes disruptive or violates the community rules. After multiple warnings about unprofessional behavior toward others in the group, and outreach that caused discomfort, we were left with no choice but to revoke your access. This decision was not made quickly or without serious consideration; it was made in alignment with our responsibility to protect the safety, integrity, and experience of all members, as well as the standards we uphold.
It’s also important to address a misunderstanding in your message. You made assumptions about Brandon and his finances. To clarify, this program was not built on wealth; it was built through sacrifice, hard work, and a mission to serve others who want to grow. Making personal assumptions about Brandon or any member of our team, especially without knowing the full context, is not only unprofessional but also inappropriate, particularly when shared publicly or with other members. According to the agreement you signed, no participant, regardless of personal hardship, is permitted to disrupt the program, breach confidentiality, or act in a way that makes continued participation unworkable. Personal attacks, unfounded claims, or mischaracterizations of our team go against the values we uphold and the professional standards we maintain at Credit Counsel Elite.
While we do understand that finances may be tough right now, the money from your enrollment was already used to support your access which includes platform costs, coaching time, support staff, tech tools, and more. That’s why your membership, like all others, is non-refundable.
At this point, we are choosing to close this matter respectfully and finally. You do not owe anything further. We will take on the financial loss, your payment plan has been canceled, and we consider your account settled. 
We truly wish you peace, growth, and success in whatever path you choose next.
Sincerely,

## 🤖 ChatGPT

Here are the **specific laws and legal principles** that make collecting payment **before presenting a contract** problematic or unenforceable:

---

### ⚖️ **1. FTC Act – Section 5 (15 U.S. Code § 45)**
- **Prohibits:** “Unfair or deceptive acts or practices in or affecting commerce.”
- **Application:** Taking payment before disclosing or presenting a binding agreement qualifies as **deceptive inducement**. The consumer cannot provide informed consent if material terms are not disclosed prior to payment.

📌 **Reference:**  
> *“A practice is deceptive if it misleads or is likely to mislead the consumer, and affects their decision or conduct regarding a product or service.”*  
> — FTC Policy Statement on Deception

---

### ⚖️ **2. Contract Law – Mutual Assent Doctrine**
- A **valid contract** requires:
  1. **Offer**
  2. **Acceptance**
  3. **Consideration**
  4. **Mutual assent (meeting of the minds)**
- **Violation:** Taking payment prior to showing terms eliminates the opportunity for informed assent, rendering the contract **voidable** or **unenforceable** under general U.S. contract law.

📌 **Restatement (Second) of Contracts §17:**  
> *“The formation of a contract requires a bargain in which there is a manifestation of mutual assent to the exchange.”*

---

### ⚖️ **3. Electronic Signatures in Global and National Commerce Act (E-SIGN Act)**
- **Requirement:** If an agreement is formed electronically, the consumer must be:
  - Clearly presented with the contract
  - Given the opportunity to review before paying
- **Violation:** Prepayment without first being shown terms is a breach of this requirement and may void consent under the act.

📌 **15 U.S. Code § 7001(c)(1)(A):**  
> *“The consumer must affirmatively consent to the use of an electronic record... after receiving a clear and conspicuous statement of the terms.”*

---

### ⚖️ **4. Truth in Lending Act (TILA) – 15 U.S. Code § 1601 et seq.**
- In cases where credit or installment payments are involved, TILA requires full **disclosure of terms prior to any payment obligation.**
- **Violation:** Accepting money before the consumer sees financing or refund terms can constitute a violation of **fair disclosure requirements.**

---

### ✅ Summary Legal Insertion (for use in your message):
> Furthermore, collecting payment before furnishing or obtaining agreement to the actual contract constitutes a deceptive and unfair practice under **15 U.S. Code § 45 (FTC Act)** and violates core tenets of **contract law**, including the **requirement of mutual assent**. It may also constitute noncompliance with the **E-SIGN Act (15 U.S. Code § 7001)** and, if applicable, the **Truth in Lending Act (15 U.S. Code § 1601)**. No party can consent to an agreement they have not been given the opportunity to review. As such, the contract is **voidable** and any “no-refund” clauses are legally unenforceable.
